# subset-b-000611 Research

Grouped source research for Linux SoC Devicetree binding YAML schemas in the Ceph client source tree. Every listed source file was read and parsed as YAML; each section preserves the source path and is wrapped for deterministic reconciliation into source-tree-aligned per-file reports.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/fsl/cpm_qe/fsl,qe.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/fsl/cpm_qe/fsl,qe.yaml` defines `Freescale QUICC Engine module (QE)`, a NXP/Freescale CPM/QE communications-engine SoC binding. This represents qe module that is installed on PowerQUICC II Pro. NOTE: This is an interim binding; it should be updated to fit in with the CPM binding later in this document. Basically, it is a bus of devices, that could act more or less as a complete entity (UCC, USB etc ). All of them should be siblings on the "root" qe node, using the common properties from there. The description below applies to the qe of MPC8360 and more nodes and properties would be extended in the future. It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. The schema uses `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/soc/fsl/cpm_qe/fsl,qe.yaml#` and meta-schema `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses ordered fallback `items` sequence with 2 tokens: `fsl,qe`, `simple-bus`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `ranges`, `model`, `bus-frequency`, `fsl,qe-num-riscs`, `fsl,qe-snums`, `fsl,firmware-phandle`, `brg-frequency`, `fsl,qe-num-snums`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `bus-frequency`; nested required-property signals include `compatible`, `reg`, `bus-frequency`. Pattern child-node APIs are `^interrupt-controller@[a-f0-9]+$`, `^muram@[a-f0-9]+$`, `^si@[a-f0-9]+$`, `^siram@[a-f0-9]+$`. Important resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `reg` describes MMIO or bus resource windows consumed during probe; `ranges` controls child address translation and bus layout. Collected limit and enum signals include `const=fsl,qe`, `const=simple-bus`, `maxItems=1`, `enum=[QE, CPM, CPM2]`, `maxItems=28`.

## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads this YAML, resolves `$ref` dependencies, validates the meta-schema, checks required properties, evaluates const/enum/items limits, applies top-level composition or conditionals (`allOf`), validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. `dtbs_check` then applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. At runtime the YAML does not execute; OF/platform code matches `compatible`, maps MMIO resources, resolves phandles, registers providers or children, and lets the matched subsystem driver own the hardware behavior.

## State and Persistence Behavior
This file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data by itself. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: compatible strings, property names, phandle cell counts, child-node names, register ordering, clock/reset names, and examples become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Runtime state belongs to the matched kernel drivers and hardware, including CPM/QE bus, UCC, MURAM, serial, Ethernet, interrupt-controller, and firmware-upload drivers; the schema only constrains how that stateful hardware is described.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Frank Li <Frank.Li@nxp.com>. Direct `$ref` dependencies are `/schemas/simple-bus.yaml#`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/string`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint8-array`, `fsl,qe-ic.yaml`, `fsl,qe-muram.yaml`, `fsl,qe-si.yaml`, `fsl,qe-siram.yaml`. Integration points include CPM/QE bus, UCC, MURAM, serial, Ethernet, interrupt-controller, and firmware-upload drivers. Compatible scans found driver-side references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/um/drivers/virtio_pcidev.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bus/imx-aipstz.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bus/simple-pm-bus.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bus/ti-sysc.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/memory/fsl_ifc.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/misc/lan966x_pci.dtso`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/of/platform.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/of/unittest-data/overlay_pci_node.dtso`, and 2 more; in-tree DTS/user references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arc/boot/dts/abilis_tb10x.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arc/boot/dts/axc001.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arc/boot/dts/axc003.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arc/boot/dts/axc003_idu.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arc/boot/dts/axs10x_mb.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arc/boot/dts/haps_hs.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arc/boot/dts/haps_hs_idu.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arc/boot/dts/hsdk.dts`, and 2 more. The source has 148 lines and 1 embedded example block.

## Risks
Primary risks are DTS ABI drift around bus child-node layout, QE clock/frequency metadata, firmware phandles, UCC numbering, and interrupt wiring; missing driver match-table updates when compatible strings change; register, interrupt, clock, reset, mailbox, memory-region, or power-domain ordering mismatches; and child-node regexes that admit malformed nodes or reject valid board descriptions. The closure profile closes composed schemas with `unevaluatedProperties: false`. Files with many compatible variants or conditionals need per-variant checks because one SoC fallback can validate while another silently loses required resources.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/fsl/cpm_qe/fsl,qe.yaml` for targeted schema validation and example compilation, then `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/fsl/cpm_qe/fsl,qe.yaml` on boards using these compatibles. High-value negative tests remove each required property, alter compatible fallback order, add an undeclared property when the schema is closed, break child-node names matched by `patternProperties`, and corrupt phandle cell counts. Runtime signals are successful probe of the matched SoC driver, expected provider registration, clean resource lookup, and no regressions in DTS users. Example seed: qe-bus@e0100000 {; compatible = "fsl,qe", "simple-bus";; reg = <0xe0100000 0x480>;; ranges = <0 0xe0100000 0x00100000>;; #address-cells = <1>;; #size-cells = <1>;; brg-frequency = <0>;; bus-frequency = <0x179a7b00>;; fsl,qe-snums = /bits/ 8 <; 0x04 0x05 0x0...

QorIQ DPAA Buffer Manager Portal Portals are memory mapped interfaces to BMan that allow low-latency, lock-less interaction by software running on processor cores, accelerators and network interfaces with the BMan It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`. Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads this YAML, resolves `$ref` dependencies, validates the meta-schema, checks required properties, evaluates const/enum/items limits, applies top-level composition or conditionals (none), validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. `dtbs_check` then applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. At runtime the YAML does not execute; OF/platform code matches `compatible`, maps MMIO resources, resolves phandles, registers providers or children, and lets the matched subsystem driver own the hardware behavior.

## State and Persistence Behavior
This file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data by itself. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: compatible strings, property names, phandle cell counts, child-node names, register ordering, clock/reset names, and examples become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Runtime state belongs to the matched kernel drivers and hardware, including DPAA BMan/QMan portal drivers, queue/buffer pool allocators, portal interrupts, and memory-backed frame-queue descriptors; the schema only constrains how that stateful hardware is described.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/fsl/fsl,bman.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/fsl/fsl,bman.yaml` defines `QorIQ DPAA Buffer Manager`, a NXP/Freescale Data Path Acceleration Architecture queue/buffer-manager binding. The Buffer Manager is part of the Data-Path Acceleration Architecture (DPAA). BMan supports hardware allocation and deallocation of buffers belonging to pools originally created by software with configurable depletion thresholds. This binding covers the CCSR space programming model It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. The schema uses `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/soc/fsl/fsl,bman.yaml#` and meta-schema `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 2 accepted forms with 3 tokens: `fsl,bman`, `fsl,ls1043a-bman`, `fsl,ls1046a-bman`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `memory-region`, `fsl,bman-portals`, `fsl,liodn`, `fsl,iommu-parent`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`; nested required-property signals include `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`. Pattern child-node APIs are none. Important resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `reg` describes MMIO or bus resource windows consumed during probe; `interrupts` maps hardware events into the IRQ domain; `memory-region` binds reserved-memory or shared-memory backing. Collected limit and enum signals include `const=fsl,bman`, `enum=[fsl,ls1043a-bman, fsl,ls1046a-bman]`, `minItems=1`, `maxItems=2`.

## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads this YAML, resolves `$ref` dependencies, validates the meta-schema, checks required properties, evaluates const/enum/items limits, applies top-level composition or conditionals (none), validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. `dtbs_check` then applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. At runtime the YAML does not execute; OF/platform code matches `compatible`, maps MMIO resources, resolves phandles, registers providers or children, and lets the matched subsystem driver own the hardware behavior.

## State and Persistence Behavior
This file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data by itself. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: compatible strings, property names, phandle cell counts, child-node names, register ordering, clock/reset names, and examples become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Runtime state belongs to the matched kernel drivers and hardware, including DPAA BMan/QMan portal drivers, queue/buffer pool allocators, portal interrupts, and memory-backed frame-queue descriptors; the schema only constrains how that stateful hardware is described.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Frank Li <Frank.Li@nxp.com>. Direct `$ref` dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32-array`. Integration points include DPAA BMan/QMan portal drivers, queue/buffer pool allocators, portal interrupts, and memory-backed frame-queue descriptors. Compatible scans found driver-side references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/iommu/fsl_pamu.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/soc/fsl/qbman/bman_ccsr.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/soc/fsl/qbman/bman_portal.c`; in-tree DTS/user references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/fsl-ls1043a.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/fsl-ls1046a.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/qoriq-bman-portals.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/qoriq-qman-portals.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/powerpc/boot/dts/fsl/b4860si-post.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/powerpc/boot/dts/fsl/b4si-post.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/powerpc/boot/dts/fsl/p1023si-post.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/powerpc/boot/dts/fsl/p2041si-post.dtsi`, and 2 more. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/fsl/fsl,imx23-digctl.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/fsl/fsl,imx23-digctl.yaml` defines `Freescale mxs digctrl for i.MX23/i.MX28`, a NXP/Freescale SoC control, reset, power-management, or syscon binding. The digital control block provides overall control of various items within the top digital block of the chip, including: - Default first-level page table (DFLPT) controls - HCLK performance counter - Free-running microseconds counter - Entropy control - BIST controls for ARM Core and On-Chip RAM - Chip Revision register - USB loop back congtrol - Other miscellaneous controls It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. The schema uses `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/soc/fsl/fsl,imx23-digctl.yaml#` and meta-schema `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 2 accepted forms with 2 tokens: `fsl,imx28-digctl`, `fsl,imx23-digctl`. ## Risks
Primary risks are DTS ABI drift around compatible fallback order, syscon register ranges, reset/power cells, clock/reset names, and SoC-specific control registers; missing driver match-table updates when compatible strings change; register, interrupt, clock, reset, mailbox, memory-region, or power-domain ordering mismatches; and child-node regexes that admit malformed nodes or reject valid board descriptions. The closure profile rejects undeclared top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. Files with many compatible variants or conditionals need per-variant checks because one SoC fallback can validate while another silently loses required resources.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/fsl/fsl,imx23-digctl.yaml` for targeted schema validation and example compilation, then `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/fsl/fsl,imx23-digctl.yaml` on boards using these compatibles. High-value negative tests remove each required property, alter compatible fallback order, add an undeclared property when the schema is closed, break child-node names matched by `patternProperties`, and corrupt phandle cell counts. Runtime signals are successful probe of the matched SoC driver, expected provider registration, clean resource lookup, and no regressions in DTS users. Example seed: digctl@8001c000 {; compatible = "fsl,imx28-digctl", "fsl,imx23-digctl";; reg = <0x8001c000 0x2000>;; interrupts = <89>;; };;

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/fsl/fsl,qman-fqd.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/fsl/fsl,qman-fqd.yaml` defines `QMan Private Memory Nodes`, a NXP/Freescale Data Path Acceleration Architecture queue/buffer-manager binding. QMan requires two contiguous range of physical memory used for the backing store for QMan Frame Queue Descriptor (FQD) and Packed Frame Descriptor Record (PFDR). This memory is reserved/allocated as a node under the /reserved-memory node. BMan requires a contiguous range of physical memory used for the backing store for BMan Free Buffer Proxy Records (FBPR). This memory is reserved/allocated as a node under the /reserved-memory node. The QMan FQD memory node must be named "qman-fqd" The QMan PFDR memory node must be named "qman-pfdr" The BMan FBPR memory node must be named "bman-fbpr" The following constraints are relevant to the FQD and PFDR private memory: - The size must be 2^(size + 1), with size = 11..29. That is 4 KiB to 1 GiB - The alignment must be a muliptle of the memory size The size of the FQD and PFDP must be chosen by observing the hardware features configured via the Re... It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. The schema uses `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/soc/fsl/fsl,qman-fqd.yaml#` and meta-schema `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses `enum` list with 3 tokens: `fsl,qman-fqd`, `fsl,qman-pfdr`, `fsl,bman-fbpr`. Top-level properties are `$nodename`, `compatible`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`; nested required-property signals include `compatible`. Pattern child-node APIs are none. Important resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry. Collected limit and enum signals include `enum=[fsl,qman-fqd, fsl,qman-pfdr, fsl,bman-fbpr]`.

## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads this YAML, resolves `$ref` dependencies, validates the meta-schema, checks required properties, evaluates const/enum/items limits, applies top-level composition or conditionals (`allOf`), validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. `dtbs_check` then applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. At runtime the YAML does not execute; OF/platform code matches `compatible`, maps MMIO resources, resolves phandles, registers providers or children, and lets the matched subsystem driver own the hardware behavior.

## State and Persistence Behavior
This file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data by itself. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: compatible strings, property names, phandle cell counts, child-node names, register ordering, clock/reset names, and examples become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Runtime signals are successful probe of the matched SoC driver, expected provider registration, clean resource lookup, and no regressions in DTS users. ## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/fsl/fsl,qman.yaml` defines `QorIQ DPAA Queue Manager`, a NXP/Freescale Data Path Acceleration Architecture queue/buffer-manager binding. The Queue Manager is part of the Data-Path Acceleration Architecture (DPAA). QMan supports queuing and QoS scheduling of frames to CPUs, network interfaces and DPAA logic modules, maintains packet ordering within flows. Besides providing flow-level queuing, is also responsible for congestion management functions such as RED/WRED, congestion notifications and tail discards. `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/imx/fsl,imx-iomuxc-gpr.yaml` defines `Freescale IOMUX Controller General Purpose Registers`, a NXP i.MX SoC bus, anatop, GPR, block-controller, or SoC-info binding. i.MX Processors have an IOMUXC General Purpose Register group for various System Settings It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. The schema uses `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/soc/imx/fsl,imx-iomuxc-gpr.yaml#` and meta-schema `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 4 accepted forms with 13 tokens: `fsl,imx6q-iomuxc-gpr`, `fsl,imx8mq-iomuxc-gpr`, `syscon`, `simple-mfd`, `fsl,imx6sl-iomuxc-gpr`, `fsl,imx6sll-iomuxc-gpr`, `fsl,imx6ul-iomuxc-gpr`, `fsl,imx6sx-iomuxc-gpr`, `fsl,imx7d-iomuxc-gpr`, `fsl,imx53-iomuxc-gpr`, `fsl,imx8mm-iomuxc-gpr`, `fsl,imx8mn-iomuxc-gpr`, `fsl,imx8mp-iomuxc-gpr`. Pattern child-node APIs are `^ipu[12]_csi[01]_mux$`. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/imx/fsl,imx8mq-vpu-blk-ctrl.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/imx/fsl,imx8mq-vpu-blk-ctrl.yaml` defines `NXP i.MX8MQ VPU blk-ctrl`, a NXP i.MX SoC bus, anatop, GPR, block-controller, or SoC-info binding. The i.MX8MQ VPU blk-ctrl is a top-level peripheral providing access to the NoC and ensuring proper power sequencing of the VPU peripherals located in the VPU domain of the SoC. It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. The schema uses `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/soc/imx/fsl,imx8mq-vpu-blk-ctrl.yaml#` and meta-schema `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses ordered fallback `items` sequence with 1 token: `fsl,imx8mq-vpu-blk-ctrl`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `#power-domain-cells`, `power-domains`, `power-domain-names`, `clocks`, `clock-names`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `power-domains`, `power-domain-names`, `clocks`, `clock-names`; nested required-property signals include `compatible`, `reg`, `power-domains`, `power-domain-names`, `clocks`, `clock-names`. Pattern child-node APIs are none. Important resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `reg` describes MMIO or bus resource windows consumed during probe; `#power-domain-cells` models generic power-domain dependencies or providers; `power-domains` models generic power-domain dependencies or providers; `power-domain-names` models generic power-domain dependencies or providers; `clocks` integrates with the clock framework; `clock-names` integrates with the clock framework. Collected limit and enum signals include `const=fsl,imx8mq-vpu-blk-ctrl`, `maxItems=1`, `const=1`, `minItems=3`, `maxItems=3`, `const=bus`, `const=g1`, `const=g2`, `minItems=2`, `maxItems=2`.

## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads this YAML, resolves `$ref` dependencies, validates the meta-schema, checks required properties, evaluates const/enum/items limits, applies top-level composition or conditionals (none), validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. `dtbs_check` then applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. At runtime the YAML does not execute; OF/platform code matches `compatible`, maps MMIO resources, resolves phandles, registers providers or children, and lets the matched subsystem driver own the hardware behavior.

## State and Persistence Behavior
This file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data by itself. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: compatible strings, property names, phandle cell counts, child-node names, register ordering, clock/reset names, and examples become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Runtime state belongs to the matched kernel drivers and hardware, including i.MX blk-ctrl, power-domain, clock, reset, syscon, display, VPU, media, HDMI, HSIO, and SoC-identification drivers; the schema only constrains how that stateful hardware is described.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Lucas Stach <l.stach@pengutronix.de>. Direct `$ref` dependencies are dt-schema core/meta schemas only. Integration points include i.MX blk-ctrl, power-domain, clock, reset, syscon, display, VPU, media, HDMI, HSIO, and SoC-identification drivers. Compatible scans found driver-side references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/pmdomain/imx/imx8m-blk-ctrl.c`; in-tree DTS/user references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/freescale/imx8mq.dtsi`. The source has 71 lines and 1 embedded example block.

## Risks
Primary risks are DTS ABI drift around power-domain cell contracts, clock/reset arrays, child bridge nodes, syscon references, and i.MX variant fallback ordering; missing driver match-table updates when compatible strings change; register, interrupt, clock, reset, mailbox, memory-region, or power-domain ordering mismatches; and child-node regexes that admit malformed nodes or reject valid board descriptions. The closure profile rejects undeclared top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. Files with many compatible variants or conditionals need per-variant checks because one SoC fallback can validate while another silently loses required resources.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/imx/fsl,imx8mq-vpu-blk-ctrl.yaml` for targeted schema validation and example compilation, then `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/imx/fsl,imx8mq-vpu-blk-ctrl.yaml` on boards using these compatibles. High-value negative tests remove each required property, alter compatible fallback order, add an undeclared property when the schema is closed, break child-node names matched by `patternProperties`, and corrupt phandle cell counts. Runtime signals are successful probe of the matched SoC driver, expected provider registration, clean resource lookup, and no regressions in DTS users. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/imx/fsl,imx93-src.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/imx/fsl,imx93-src.yaml` defines `NXP i.MX93 System Reset Controller`, a NXP i.MX SoC bus, anatop, GPR, block-controller, or SoC-info binding. The System Reset Controller (SRC) is responsible for the generation of all the system reset signals and boot argument latching. Its main functions are as follows, - Deals with all global system reset sources from other modules, and generates global system reset. - Responsible for power gating of MIXs (Slices) and their memory low power control. It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/intel/intel,hps-copy-engine.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/intel/intel,hps-copy-engine.yaml` defines `Intel HPS Copy Engine`, a Intel SoC syscon or HPS copy-engine binding. The Intel Hard Processor System (HPS) Copy Engine is an IP block used to copy a bootable image from host memory to HPS DDR. Additionally, there is a register the HPS can use to indicate the state of booting the copied image as well as a keep-a-live indication to the host. Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads this YAML, resolves `$ref` dependencies, validates the meta-schema, checks required properties, evaluates const/enum/items limits, applies top-level composition or conditionals (none), validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. `dtbs_check` then applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: compatible strings, property names, phandle cell counts, child-node names, register ordering, clock/reset names, and examples become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Runtime state belongs to the matched kernel drivers and hardware, including MediaTek MMSYS/display pipeline, mutex, WDMA, CCORR, PMIC wrapper, DVFSRC, SVS, device-APC, and Wo CCIF drivers; the schema only constrains how that stateful hardware is described.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Matthias Brugger <matthias.bgg@gmail.com>, Moudy Ho <moudy.ho@mediatek.com>. Direct `$ref` dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle-array`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32-array`. Integration points include MediaTek MMSYS/display pipeline, mutex, WDMA, CCORR, PMIC wrapper, DVFSRC, SVS, device-APC, and Wo CCIF drivers. Compatible scans found driver-side references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/media/platform/mediatek/mdp3/mtk-mdp3-comp.c`; in-tree DTS/user references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/mediatek/mt8183.dtsi`. The source has 68 lines and 1 embedded example block.

## Risks
Primary risks are DTS ABI drift around GCE mailbox links, power/clock/reset resources, register-bank layout, display component routing, OPP/supply constraints, and SoC-specific compatible variants; missing driver match-table updates when compatible strings change; register, interrupt, clock, reset, mailbox, memory-region, or power-domain ordering mismatches; and child-node regexes that admit malformed nodes or reject valid board descriptions. The closure profile rejects undeclared top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. Files with many compatible variants or conditionals need per-variant checks because one SoC fallback can validate while another silently loses required resources.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/mediatek/mediatek,ccorr.yaml` for targeted schema validation and example compilation, then `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/mediatek/mediatek,ccorr.yaml` on boards using these compatibles. High-value negative tests remove each required property, alter compatible fallback order, add an undeclared property when the schema is closed, break child-node names matched by `patternProperties`, and corrupt phandle cell counts. Runtime signals are successful probe of the matched SoC driver, expected provider registration, clean resource lookup, and no regressions in DTS users. Example seed: #include <dt-bindings/clock/mt8183-clk.h>; #include <dt-bindings/gce/mt8183-gce.h>; ; mdp3_ccorr: mdp3-ccorr@1401c000 {; compatible = "mediatek,mt8183-mdp3-ccorr";; reg = <0x1401c000 0x1000>;; mediatek,gce-client-reg = <&gce SUBSYS_1401XXXX 0xc000 0x1000>;;...

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/mediatek/mediatek,mt7986-wo-ccif.yaml` defines `MediaTek Wireless Ethernet Dispatch (WED) WO controller interface for MT7986`, a MediaTek SoC display, security, power, PMIC-wrapper, DVFS, or voltage-scaling binding. The MediaTek wo-ccif provides a configuration interface for WED WO controller used to perform offload rx packet processing (e.g. 802.11 aggregation packet reordering or rx header translation) on MT7986 soc. It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/mediatek/mediatek,mt8183-dvfsrc.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/mediatek/mediatek,mt8183-dvfsrc.yaml` defines `MediaTek Dynamic Voltage and Frequency Scaling Resource Collector (DVFSRC)`, a MediaTek SoC display, security, power, PMIC-wrapper, DVFS, or voltage-scaling binding. The Dynamic Voltage and Frequency Scaling Resource Collector (DVFSRC) is a Hardware module used to collect all the requests from both software and the various remote processors embedded into the SoC and decide about a minimum operating voltage and a minimum DRAM frequency to fulfill those requests in an effort to provide the best achievable performance per watt. This hardware IP is capable of transparently performing direct register R/W on all of the DVFSRC-controlled regulators and SoC bandwidth knobs. It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. The schema uses `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/soc/mediatek/mediatek,mt8183-dvfsrc.yaml#` and meta-schema `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 2 accepted forms with 4 tokens: `mediatek,mt6893-dvfsrc`, `mediatek,mt8183-dvfsrc`, `mediatek,mt8195-dvfsrc`, `mediatek,mt8192-dvfsrc`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `regulators`, `interconnect`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; nested required-property signals include `compatible`, `reg`. Pattern child-node APIs are none. Important resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `reg` describes MMIO or bus resource windows consumed during probe; `clocks` integrates with the clock framework. Collected limit and enum signals include `enum=[mediatek,mt6893-dvfsrc, mediatek,mt8183-dvfsrc, mediatek,mt8195-dvfsrc]`, `const=mediatek,mt8192-dvfsrc`, `const=mediatek,mt8195-dvfsrc`, `maxItems=1`.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/mediatek/mediatek,mutex.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/mediatek/mediatek,mutex.yaml` defines `Mediatek mutex`, a MediaTek SoC display, security, power, PMIC-wrapper, DVFS, or voltage-scaling binding. Mediatek mutex, namely MUTEX, is used to send the triggers signals called Start Of Frame (SOF) / End Of Frame (EOF) to each sub-modules on the display data path or MDP data path. In some SoC, such as mt2701, MUTEX could be a hardware mutex which protects the shadow register. MUTEX device node must be siblings to the central MMSYS_CONFIG node. For a description of the MMSYS_CONFIG binding, see Documentation/devicetree/bindings/arm/mediatek/mediatek,mmsys.yaml for details. It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `interrupts`, `power-domains`, `clocks`, `mediatek,gce-events`, `mediatek,gce-client-reg`. Pattern child-node APIs are none. Important resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `reg` describes MMIO or bus resource windows consumed during probe; `interrupts` maps hardware events into the IRQ domain; `power-domains` models generic power-domain dependencies or providers; `clocks` integrates with the clock framework. Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads this YAML, resolves `$ref` dependencies, validates the meta-schema, checks required properties, evaluates const/enum/items limits, applies top-level composition or conditionals (`allOf`), validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. `dtbs_check` then applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/mediatek/mediatek,pwrap.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/mediatek/mediatek,pwrap.yaml` defines `Mediatek PMIC Wrapper`, a MediaTek SoC display, security, power, PMIC-wrapper, DVFS, or voltage-scaling binding. On MediaTek SoCs the PMIC is connected via SPI. The SPI master interface is not directly visible to the CPU, but only through the PMIC wrapper inside the SoC. The communication between the SoC and the PMIC can optionally be encrypted. Also a non standard Dual IO SPI mode can be used to increase speed. IP Pairing On MT8135 the pins of some SoC internal peripherals can be on the PMIC. The signals of these pins are routed over the SPI bus using the pwrap bridge. In the binding description below the properties needed for bridging are marked with "IP Pairing". These are optional on SoCs which do not support IP Pairing It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `reg-names`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `resets`, `reset-names`, `power-domains`, `pmic`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `reg-names`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `clock-names`; nested required-property signals include `compatible`, `reg`, `reg-names`, `interrupts`, `clocks`, `clock-names`. Pattern child-node APIs are none. Important resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `reg` describes MMIO or bus resource windows consumed during probe; `reg-names` fixes the semantic order of multiple register windows; `interrupts` maps hardware events into the IRQ domain; `clocks` integrates with the clock framework; `clock-names` integrates with the clock framework; `resets` integrates with reset-controller providers or consumers; `reset-names` integrates with reset-controller providers or consumers; `power-domains` models generic power-domain dependencies or providers. Collected limit and enum signals include `enum=[mediatek,mt2701-pwrap, mediatek,mt6765-pwrap, mediatek,mt6779-pwrap, mediatek,mt6795-pwrap, mediatek,mt6797-pwrap, mediatek,mt6873-pwrap, mediatek,mt7622-pwrap, mediatek,mt8135-pwrap, ...]`, `enum=[mediatek,mt8186-pwrap, mediatek,mt8195-pwrap]`, `const=syscon`, `enum=[mediatek,mt8188-pwrap, mediatek,mt8189-pwrap]`, `const=mediatek,mt8195-pwrap`, `minItems=1`, `const=pwrap`, `const=pwrap-bridge`, `maxItems=1`, `minItems=2`, `const=spi`, `const=wrap`, `const=sys`, `const=tmr`, `const=mediatek,mt8365-pwrap`, `minItems=4`, `const=mediatek,mt8173-pwrap`.

## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads this YAML, resolves `$ref` dependencies, validates the meta-schema, checks required properties, evaluates const/enum/items limits, applies top-level composition or conditionals (`allOf`, `dependentRequired`), validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. `dtbs_check` then applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/mediatek/mtk-svs.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/mediatek/mtk-svs.yaml` defines `MediaTek Smart Voltage Scaling (SVS)`, a MediaTek SoC display, security, power, PMIC-wrapper, DVFS, or voltage-scaling binding. The SVS engine is a piece of hardware which has several controllers(banks) for calculating suitable voltage to different power domains(CPU/GPU/CCI) according to chip process corner, temperatures and other factors. Then DVFS driver could apply SVS bank voltage to PMIC/Buck. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/microchip/microchip,mpfs-irqmux.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/microchip/microchip,mpfs-irqmux.yaml` defines `Microchip Polarfire SoC GPIO Interrupt Mux`, a Microchip/Atmel SoC timer, syscon, interrupt-mux, or system-controller binding. There are 3 GPIO controllers on this SoC, of which: - GPIO controller 0 has 14 GPIOs - GPIO controller 1 has 24 GPIOs - GPIO controller 2 has 32 GPIOs All GPIOs are capable of generating interrupts, for a total of 70. There are only 41 IRQs available however, so a configurable mux is used to ensure all GPIOs can be used for interrupt generation. 38 of the 41 interrupts are in what the documentation calls "direct mode", as they provide an exclusive connection from a GPIO to the PLIC. Lines 18 to 23 on GPIO controller 1 are always in "direct mode". The 3 remaining interrupts are used to mux the interrupts which do not have a exclusive connection, one for each GPIO controller. It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. The schema uses `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/soc/microchip/microchip,mpfs-irqmux.yaml#` and meta-schema `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses single `const` with 1 token: `microchip,mpfs-irqmux`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `#address-cells`, `#interrupt-cells`, `interrupt-map-mask`, `interrupt-map`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `#address-cells`, `#interrupt-cells`, `interrupt-map-mask`, `interrupt-map`; nested required-property signals include `compatible`, `reg`, `#address-cells`, `#interrupt-cells`, `interrupt-map-mask`, `interrupt-map`. Pattern child-node APIs are none. Important resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `reg` describes MMIO or bus resource windows consumed during probe; `#address-cells` controls child address translation and bus layout; `#interrupt-cells` exposes this node as a phandle provider. Collected limit and enum signals include `const=microchip,mpfs-irqmux`, `maxItems=1`, `const=0`, `const=1`, `const=127`, `maxItems=70`.

## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads this YAML, resolves `$ref` dependencies, validates the meta-schema, checks required properties, evaluates const/enum/items limits, applies top-level composition or conditionals (none), validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. `dtbs_check` then applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. At runtime the YAML does not execute; OF/platform code matches `compatible`, maps MMIO resources, resolves phandles, registers providers or children, and lets the matched subsystem driver own the hardware behavior.

## State and Persistence Behavior
This file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data by itself. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: compatible strings, property names, phandle cell counts, child-node names, register ordering, clock/reset names, and examples become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Runtime state belongs to the matched kernel drivers and hardware, including AT91 TCB timer/counter, PolarFire SoC sys-controller, IRQ mux, mailbox, reset, and Sparx5 CPU syscon drivers; the schema only constrains how that stateful hardware is described.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Conor Dooley <conor.dooley@microchip.com>. Direct `$ref` dependencies are dt-schema core/meta schemas only. ## Purpose

This file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data by itself. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: compatible strings, property names, phandle cell counts, child-node names, register ordering, clock/reset names, and examples become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. ## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/microchip/microchip,mpfs-sys-controller.yaml` defines `Microchip PolarFire SoC (MPFS) MSS (microprocessor subsystem) system controller`, a Microchip/Atmel SoC timer, syscon, interrupt-mux, or system-controller binding. PolarFire SoC devices include a microcontroller acting as the system controller, which provides "services" to the main processor and to the FPGA fabric. These services include hardware rng, reprogramming of the FPGA and verification of the eNVM contents etc. More information on these services can be found online, at https://onlinedocs.microchip.com/pr/GUID-1409CF11-8EF9-4C24-A94E-70979A688632-en-US-1/index.html Communication with the system controller is done via a mailbox, of which the client portion is documented here. ## State and Persistence Behavior
This file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data by itself. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: compatible strings, property names, phandle cell counts, child-node names, register ordering, clock/reset names, and examples become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Runtime state belongs to the matched kernel drivers and hardware, including AT91 TCB timer/counter, PolarFire SoC sys-controller, IRQ mux, mailbox, reset, and Sparx5 CPU syscon drivers; the schema only constrains how that stateful hardware is described.

Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/mobileye/mobileye,eyeq5-olb.yaml` for targeted schema validation and example compilation, then `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/mobileye/mobileye,eyeq5-olb.yaml` on boards using these compatibles. High-value negative tests remove each required property, alter compatible fallback order, add an undeclared property when the schema is closed, break child-node names matched by `patternProperties`, and corrupt phandle cell counts. Runtime signals are successful probe of the matched SoC driver, expected provider registration, clean resource lookup, and no regressions in DTS users. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/mobileye/mobileye,eyeq6lplus-olb.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/mobileye/mobileye,eyeq6lplus-olb.yaml` defines `Mobileye EyeQ6Lplus SoC system controller`, a Mobileye EyeQ OLB system-controller binding. OLB ("Other Logic Block") is a hardware block grouping smaller blocks. Clocks, resets, pinctrl are being handled from here. EyeQ6Lplus hosts a single instance providing 22 clocks, two reset domains and one bank of 32 pins. It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. The schema uses `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/soc/mobileye/mobileye,eyeq6lplus-olb.yaml#` and meta-schema `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses ordered fallback `items` sequence with 2 tokens: `mobileye,eyeq6lplus-olb`, `syscon`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `#reset-cells`, `#clock-cells`, `clocks`, `clock-names`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `#clock-cells`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `#reset-cells`; nested required-property signals include `pins`, `function`, `compatible`, `reg`, `#clock-cells`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `#reset-cells`. Pattern child-node APIs are `-pins?$`. Important resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `reg` describes MMIO or bus resource windows consumed during probe; `#reset-cells` integrates with reset-controller providers or consumers; `#clock-cells` integrates with the clock framework; `clocks` integrates with the clock framework; `clock-names` integrates with the clock framework. Collected limit and enum signals include `const=mobileye,eyeq6lplus-olb`, `const=syscon`, `maxItems=1`, `const=2`, `const=1`, `const=ref`, `enum=[gpio, timer0, timer1, uart_ssi, spi0, uart0, timer2, timer3, ...]`, `const=gpio`, `const=timer0`, `enum=[PA0, PA1]`, `const=timer1`, `enum=[PA2, PA3]`, `const=uart_ssi`, `enum=[PA4, PA5]`, `const=spi0`, `enum=[PA6, PA7, PA8, PA9, PA10]`, `const=uart0`, `enum=[PA11, PA12]`, `const=timer2`, `enum=[PA13, PA14]`, and 12 more.

## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads this YAML, resolves `$ref` dependencies, validates the meta-schema, checks required properties, evaluates const/enum/items limits, applies top-level composition or conditionals (none), validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. `dtbs_check` then applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. At runtime the YAML does not execute; OF/platform code matches `compatible`, maps MMIO resources, resolves phandles, registers providers or children, and lets the matched subsystem driver own the hardware behavior.

## State and Persistence Behavior
This file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data by itself. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: compatible strings, property names, phandle cell counts, child-node names, register ordering, clock/reset names, and examples become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Runtime state belongs to the matched kernel drivers and hardware, including EyeQ OLB syscon, pinctrl, reset, clock, and SoC control drivers; the schema only constrains how that stateful hardware is described.

## Dependencies and Integration Points

## Risks
Primary risks are DTS ABI drift around OLB register windows, compatible variants, child controller layout, and syscon-backed service boundaries; missing driver match-table updates when compatible strings change; register, interrupt, clock, reset, mailbox, memory-region, or power-domain ordering mismatches; and child-node regexes that admit malformed nodes or reject valid board descriptions. Runtime signals are successful probe of the matched SoC driver, expected provider registration, clean resource lookup, and no regressions in DTS users. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,aoss-qmp.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,aoss-qmp.yaml` defines `Qualcomm Always-On Subsystem side channel`, a Qualcomm SoC inter-processor, power/resource-management, debug, or serial-engine binding. This binding describes the hardware component responsible for side channel requests to the always-on subsystem (AOSS), used for certain power management requests that is not handled by the standard RPMh interface. Each client in the SoC has its own block of message RAM and IRQ for communication with the AOSS. The protocol used to communicate in the message RAM is known as Qualcomm Messaging Protocol (QMP) The AOSS side channel exposes control over a set of resources, used to control a set of debug related clocks and to affect the low power state of resources related to the secondary subsystems. It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. Pattern child-node APIs are `^(cx|mx|ebi)$`. Important resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `reg` describes MMIO or bus resource windows consumed during probe; `interrupts` maps hardware events into the IRQ domain; `mboxes` links the node to mailbox transport channels; `#clock-cells` integrates with the clock framework. Collected limit and enum signals include `enum=[qcom,glymur-aoss-qmp, qcom,kaanapali-aoss-qmp, qcom,milos-aoss-qmp, qcom,qcs615-aoss-qmp, qcom,qcs8300-aoss-qmp, qcom,qdu1000-aoss-qmp, qcom,sa8255p-aoss-qmp, qcom,sa8775p-aoss-qmp, ...]`, `const=qcom,aoss-qmp`, `maxItems=1`, `const=0`, `const=2`.

## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads this YAML, resolves `$ref` dependencies, validates the meta-schema, checks required properties, evaluates const/enum/items limits, applies top-level composition or conditionals (none), validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. `dtbs_check` then applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. At runtime the YAML does not execute; OF/platform code matches `compatible`, maps MMIO resources, resolves phandles, registers providers or children, and lets the matched subsystem driver own the hardware behavior.

## State and Persistence Behavior
This file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data by itself. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: compatible strings, property names, phandle cell counts, child-node names, register ordering, clock/reset names, and examples become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. ## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,geni-se.yaml` defines `GENI Serial Engine QUP Wrapper Controller`, a Qualcomm SoC inter-processor, power/resource-management, debug, or serial-engine binding. Generic Interface (GENI) based Qualcomm Universal Peripheral (QUP) wrapper is a programmable module for supporting a wide range of serial interfaces like UART, SPI, I2C, I3C, etc. A single QUP module can provide upto 8 Serial Interfaces, using its internal Serial Engines. The GENI Serial Engine QUP Wrapper controller is modeled as a node with zero or more child nodes each representing a serial engine. ## Risks
Primary risks are DTS ABI drift around remoteproc/channel child nodes, mbox/interrupt semantics, shared-memory regions, RPM/RPMh resources, service IDs, clock/reset names, and compatible-specific branches; missing driver match-table updates when compatible strings change; register, interrupt, clock, reset, mailbox, memory-region, or power-domain ordering mismatches; and child-node regexes that admit malformed nodes or reject valid board descriptions. Files with many compatible variants or conditionals need per-variant checks because one SoC fallback can validate while another silently loses required resources.

High-value negative tests remove each required property, alter compatible fallback order, add an undeclared property when the schema is closed, break child-node names matched by `patternProperties`, and corrupt phandle cell counts. Runtime signals are successful probe of the matched SoC driver, expected provider registration, clean resource lookup, and no regressions in DTS users. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,gsbi.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,gsbi.yaml` defines `Qualcomm General Serial Bus Interface (GSBI)`, a Qualcomm SoC inter-processor, power/resource-management, debug, or serial-engine binding. The GSBI controller is modeled as a node with zero or more child nodes, each representing a serial sub-node device that is mux'd as part of the GSBI configuration settings. The mode setting will govern the input/output mode of the 4 GSBI IOs. A GSBI controller node can contain 0 or more child nodes representing serial devices. These serial devices can be a QCOM UART, I2C controller, spi controller, or some combination of aforementioned devices. It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. The schema uses `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/soc/qcom/qcom,gsbi.yaml#` and meta-schema `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses single `const` with 1 token: `qcom,gsbi-v1.0.0`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `#address-cells`, `cell-index`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `qcom,crci`, `qcom,mode`, `#size-cells`, `syscon-tcsr`, `ranges`, `reg`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `cell-index`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `qcom,mode`, `reg`; nested required-property signals include `compatible`, `cell-index`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `qcom,mode`, `reg`. Pattern child-node APIs are `i2c@[0-9a-f]+$`, `serial@[0-9a-f]+$`, `spi@[0-9a-f]+$`. Important resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `#address-cells` controls child address translation and bus layout; `clocks` integrates with the clock framework; `clock-names` integrates with the clock framework; `#size-cells` controls child address translation and bus layout; `ranges` controls child address translation and bus layout; `reg` describes MMIO or bus resource windows consumed during probe. Collected limit and enum signals include `const=qcom,gsbi-v1.0.0`, `const=1`, `maxItems=1`, `const=iface`.

## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads this YAML, resolves `$ref` dependencies, validates the meta-schema, checks required properties, evaluates const/enum/items limits, applies top-level composition or conditionals (none), validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. `dtbs_check` then applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. At runtime the YAML does not execute; OF/platform code matches `compatible`, maps MMIO resources, resolves phandles, registers providers or children, and lets the matched subsystem driver own the hardware behavior.

## State and Persistence Behavior
This file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data by itself. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: compatible strings, property names, phandle cell counts, child-node names, register ordering, clock/reset names, and examples become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Runtime state belongs to the matched kernel drivers and hardware, including Qualcomm RPM/RPMh, SMD, SMEM, SMP2P, SMSM, APR, GLINK, AOSS, GENI/QUP, GSBI, DCC, EUD, WCNSS, power, clock, interconnect, and mailbox drivers; the schema only constrains how that stateful hardware is described.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,rpm-master-stats.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,rpm-master-stats.yaml` defines `Qualcomm Technologies, Inc. (QTI) RPM Master Stats`, a Qualcomm SoC inter-processor, power/resource-management, debug, or serial-engine binding. The Qualcomm RPM (Resource Power Manager) architecture includes a concept of "RPM Masters". They can be thought of as "the local gang leaders", usually spanning a single subsystem (e.g. APSS, ADSP, CDSP). All of the RPM decisions (particularly around entering hardware-driven low power modes: XO shutdown and total system-wide power collapse) are first made at Master-level, and only then aggregated for the entire system. The Master Stats provide a few useful bits that can be used to assess whether our device has entered the desired low-power mode, how long it took to do so, the duration of that residence, how long it took to come back online, how many times a given sleep state was entered and which cores are actively voting for staying awake. This scheme has been used on various SoCs in the 2013-2023 era, with some newer or higher-end designs providing this information through an SMEM q... It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. The schema uses `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/soc/qcom/qcom,rpm-master-stats.yaml#` and meta-schema `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses single `const` with 1 token: `qcom,rpm-master-stats`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `qcom,rpm-msg-ram`, `qcom,master-names`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `qcom,rpm-msg-ram`, `qcom,master-names`; nested required-property signals include `compatible`, `qcom,rpm-msg-ram`, `qcom,master-names`. Pattern child-node APIs are none. Important resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry. Collected limit and enum signals include `const=qcom,rpm-master-stats`, `minItems=1`, `maxItems=5`, `maxItems=1`.

## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads this YAML, resolves `$ref` dependencies, validates the meta-schema, checks required properties, evaluates const/enum/items limits, applies top-level composition or conditionals (none), validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. `dtbs_check` then applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. At runtime the YAML does not execute; OF/platform code matches `compatible`, maps MMIO resources, resolves phandles, registers providers or children, and lets the matched subsystem driver own the hardware behavior.

## State and Persistence Behavior
This file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data by itself. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: compatible strings, property names, phandle cell counts, child-node names, register ordering, clock/reset names, and examples become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Runtime state belongs to the matched kernel drivers and hardware, including Qualcomm RPM/RPMh, SMD, SMEM, SMP2P, SMSM, APR, GLINK, AOSS, GENI/QUP, GSBI, DCC, EUD, WCNSS, power, clock, interconnect, and mailbox drivers; the schema only constrains how that stateful hardware is described.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Konrad Dybcio <konradybcio@kernel.org>. Direct `$ref` dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle-array`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/string-array`. Integration points include Qualcomm RPM/RPMh, SMD, SMEM, SMP2P, SMSM, APR, GLINK, AOSS, GENI/QUP, GSBI, DCC, EUD, WCNSS, power, clock, interconnect, and mailbox drivers. Compatible scans found driver-side references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/soc/qcom/rpm_master_stats.c`; in-tree DTS/user references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/qcom/qcom-msm8226.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/qcom/qcom-msm8974.dtsi`. The source has 71 lines and 1 embedded example block.

## Risks
Primary risks are DTS ABI drift around remoteproc/channel child nodes, mbox/interrupt semantics, shared-memory regions, RPM/RPMh resources, service IDs, clock/reset names, and compatible-specific branches; missing driver match-table updates when compatible strings change; register, interrupt, clock, reset, mailbox, memory-region, or power-domain ordering mismatches; and child-node regexes that admit malformed nodes or reject valid board descriptions. The closure profile rejects undeclared top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. Files with many compatible variants or conditionals need per-variant checks because one SoC fallback can validate while another silently loses required resources.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,rpm-master-stats.yaml` for targeted schema validation and example compilation, then `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,rpm-master-stats.yaml` on boards using these compatibles. High-value negative tests remove each required property, alter compatible fallback order, add an undeclared property when the schema is closed, break child-node names matched by `patternProperties`, and corrupt phandle cell counts. Runtime signals are successful probe of the matched SoC driver, expected provider registration, clean resource lookup, and no regressions in DTS users. Example seed: stats {; compatible = "qcom,rpm-master-stats";; qcom,rpm-msg-ram = <&apss_master_stats>,; <&mpss_master_stats>,; <&adsp_master_stats>,; <&cdsp_master_stats>,; <&tz_master_stats>;; qcom,master-names = "APSS",; "MPSS",; "ADSP",; "CDSP",; "TZ";; };;
<!-- END_FILE_RESEARCH: sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,rpm-master-stats.yaml -->

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,rpm.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,rpm.yaml` defines `Qualcomm Resource Power Manager (RPM)`, a Qualcomm SoC inter-processor, power/resource-management, debug, or serial-engine binding. This driver is used to interface with the Resource Power Manager (RPM) found in various Qualcomm platforms. The RPM allows each component in the system to vote for state of the system resources, such as clocks, regulators and bus frequencies. It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,rpmh-rsc.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,rpmh-rsc.yaml` defines `Qualcomm RPMH RSC`, a Qualcomm SoC inter-processor, power/resource-management, debug, or serial-engine binding. Resource Power Manager Hardened (RPMH) is the mechanism for communicating with the hardened resource accelerators on Qualcomm SoCs. Requests to the resources can be written to the Trigger Command Set (TCS) registers and using a (addr, val) pair and triggered. Messages in the TCS are then sent in sequence over an internal bus. The hardware block (Direct Resource Voter or DRV) is a part of the h/w entity (Resource State Coordinator a.k.a RSC) that can handle multiple sleep and active/wake resource requests. Multiple such DRVs can exist in a SoC and can be written to from Linux. The structure of each DRV follows the same template with a few variations that are captured by the properties here. A TCS may be triggered from Linux or triggered by the F/W after all the CPUs have powered off to facilitate idle power saving. TCS could be classified as:: ACTIVE - Triggered by Linux SLEEP - Trigge... It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. The schema uses `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/soc/qcom/qcom,rpmh-rsc.yaml#` and meta-schema `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 2 accepted forms with 3 tokens: `qcom,sc7180-rpmh-apps-rsc`, `qcom,sdm845-rpmh-apps-rsc`, `qcom,rpmh-rsc`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `interrupts`, `label`, `qcom,drv-id`, `qcom,tcs-config`, `qcom,tcs-offset`, `reg`, `reg-names`, `power-domains`, `bcm-voter`, `clock-controller`, `power-controller`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `interrupts`, `qcom,drv-id`, `qcom,tcs-config`, `qcom,tcs-offset`, `reg`, `reg-names`; nested required-property signals include `compatible`, `interrupts`, `qcom,drv-id`, `qcom,tcs-config`, `qcom,tcs-offset`, `reg`, `reg-names`, `power-domains`. Pattern child-node APIs are `^regulators(-[0-9])?$`. Important resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `interrupts` maps hardware events into the IRQ domain; `reg` describes MMIO or bus resource windows consumed during probe; `reg-names` fixes the semantic order of multiple register windows; `power-domains` models generic power-domain dependencies or providers. Collected limit and enum signals include `enum=[qcom,sc7180-rpmh-apps-rsc, qcom,sdm845-rpmh-apps-rsc]`, `const=qcom,rpmh-rsc`, `minItems=1`, `maxItems=4`, `minItems=4`, `enum=[0, 1, 2, 3]`, `const=drv-0`, `const=drv-1`, `const=drv-2`, `const=drv-3`, `maxItems=1`.

## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads this YAML, resolves `$ref` dependencies, validates the meta-schema, checks required properties, evaluates const/enum/items limits, applies top-level composition or conditionals (`allOf`), validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. `dtbs_check` then applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. At runtime the YAML does not execute; OF/platform code matches `compatible`, maps MMIO resources, resolves phandles, registers providers or children, and lets the matched subsystem driver own the hardware behavior.

## State and Persistence Behavior
This file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data by itself. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: compatible strings, property names, phandle cell counts, child-node names, register ordering, clock/reset names, and examples become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Runtime state belongs to the matched kernel drivers and hardware, including Qualcomm RPM/RPMh, SMD, SMEM, SMP2P, SMSM, APR, GLINK, AOSS, GENI/QUP, GSBI, DCC, EUD, WCNSS, power, clock, interconnect, and mailbox drivers; the schema only constrains how that stateful hardware is described.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Bjorn Andersson <bjorn.andersson@linaro.org>. Direct `$ref` dependencies are `/schemas/clock/qcom,rpmhcc.yaml#`, `/schemas/interconnect/qcom,bcm-voter.yaml#`, `/schemas/power/qcom,rpmpd.yaml#`, `/schemas/regulator/qcom,rpmh-regulator.yaml#`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/uint32-matrix`. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,sa8255p-geni-se-qup.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,sa8255p-geni-se-qup.yaml` defines `GENI Serial Engine QUP Wrapper Controller`, a Qualcomm SoC inter-processor, power/resource-management, debug, or serial-engine binding. Generic Interface (GENI) based Qualcomm Universal Peripheral (QUP) wrapper is a programmable module for supporting a wide range of serial interfaces like UART, SPI, I2C, I3C, etc. A single QUP module can provide up to 8 Serial Interfaces, using its internal Serial Engines. The GENI Serial Engine QUP Wrapper controller is modeled as a node with zero or more child nodes each representing a serial engine. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,saw2.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,saw2.yaml` defines `Qualcomm Subsystem Power Manager / SPM AVS Wrapper 2 (SAW2)`, a Qualcomm SoC inter-processor, power/resource-management, debug, or serial-engine binding. The Qualcomm Subsystem Power Manager is used to control the peripheral logic surrounding the application cores in Qualcomm platforms. The SAW2 is a wrapper around the Subsystem Power Manager (SPM) and the Adaptive Voltage Scaling (AVS) hardware. The SPM is a programmable power-controller that transitions a piece of hardware (like a processor or subsystem) into and out of low power modes via a direct connection to the PMIC. It can also be wired up to interact with other processors in the system, notifying them when a low power state is entered or exited. It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. The schema uses `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/soc/qcom/qcom,saw2.yaml#` and meta-schema `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses ordered fallback `items` sequence with 21 tokens: `qcom,ipq4019-saw2-cpu`, `qcom,ipq4019-saw2-l2`, `qcom,ipq8064-saw2-cpu`, `qcom,sdm660-gold-saw2-v4.1-l2`, `qcom,sdm660-silver-saw2-v4.1-l2`, `qcom,msm8998-gold-saw2-v4.1-l2`, `qcom,msm8998-silver-saw2-v4.1-l2`, `qcom,msm8909-saw2-v3.0-cpu`, `qcom,msm8916-saw2-v3.0-cpu`, `qcom,msm8939-saw2-v3.0-cpu`, `qcom,msm8226-saw2-v2.1-cpu`, `qcom,msm8226-saw2-v2.1-l2`, `qcom,msm8960-saw2-cpu`, `qcom,msm8974-saw2-v2.1-cpu`, `qcom,msm8974-saw2-v2.1-l2`, `qcom,msm8976-gold-saw2-v2.3-l2`, `qcom,msm8976-silver-saw2-v2.3-l2`, `qcom,apq8084-saw2-v2.1-cpu`, `qcom,apq8084-saw2-v2.1-l2`, `qcom,apq8064-saw2-v1.1-cpu`, `qcom,saw2`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `regulator`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; nested required-property signals include `compatible`, `reg`. Pattern child-node APIs are none. Important resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `reg` describes MMIO or bus resource windows consumed during probe. Collected limit and enum signals include `enum=[qcom,ipq4019-saw2-cpu, qcom,ipq4019-saw2-l2, qcom,ipq8064-saw2-cpu, qcom,sdm660-gold-saw2-v4.1-l2, qcom,sdm660-silver-saw2-v4.1-l2, qcom,msm8998-gold-saw2-v4.1-l2, qcom,msm8998-silver-saw2-v4.1-l2, qcom,msm8909-saw2-v3.0-cpu, ...]`, `const=qcom,saw2`, `minItems=1`.

## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads this YAML, resolves `$ref` dependencies, validates the meta-schema, checks required properties, evaluates const/enum/items limits, applies top-level composition or conditionals (none), validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. `dtbs_check` then applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. At runtime the YAML does not execute; OF/platform code matches `compatible`, maps MMIO resources, resolves phandles, registers providers or children, and lets the matched subsystem driver own the hardware behavior.

## State and Persistence Behavior
This file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data by itself. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: compatible strings, property names, phandle cell counts, child-node names, register ordering, clock/reset names, and examples become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Runtime state belongs to the matched kernel drivers and hardware, including Qualcomm RPM/RPMh, SMD, SMEM, SMP2P, SMSM, APR, GLINK, AOSS, GENI/QUP, GSBI, DCC, EUD, WCNSS, power, clock, interconnect, and mailbox drivers; the schema only constrains how that stateful hardware is described.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Andy Gross <agross@kernel.org>, Bjorn Andersson <bjorn.andersson@linaro.org>. Direct `$ref` dependencies are `/schemas/regulator/regulator.yaml#`. Integration points include Qualcomm RPM/RPMh, SMD, SMEM, SMP2P, SMSM, APR, GLINK, AOSS, GENI/QUP, GSBI, DCC, EUD, WCNSS, power, clock, interconnect, and mailbox drivers. Compatible scans found driver-side references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/soc/qcom/spm.c`; in-tree DTS/user references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/qcom/qcom-apq8064.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/qcom/qcom-ipq4019.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/qcom/qcom-ipq8064.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/qcom/qcom-msm8226.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/qcom/qcom-msm8960.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/qcom/qcom-msm8974.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/msm8916.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/msm8939.dtsi`. The source has 120 lines and 3 embedded example blocks.

## Risks
Primary risks are DTS ABI drift around remoteproc/channel child nodes, mbox/interrupt semantics, shared-memory regions, RPM/RPMh resources, service IDs, clock/reset names, and compatible-specific branches; missing driver match-table updates when compatible strings change; register, interrupt, clock, reset, mailbox, memory-region, or power-domain ordering mismatches; and child-node regexes that admit malformed nodes or reject valid board descriptions. The closure profile rejects undeclared top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. Files with many compatible variants or conditionals need per-variant checks because one SoC fallback can validate while another silently loses required resources.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,saw2.yaml` for targeted schema validation and example compilation, then `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,saw2.yaml` on boards using these compatibles. High-value negative tests remove each required property, alter compatible fallback order, add an undeclared property when the schema is closed, break child-node names matched by `patternProperties`, and corrupt phandle cell counts. Runtime signals are successful probe of the matched SoC driver, expected provider registration, clean resource lookup, and no regressions in DTS users. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,smd-rpm.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,smd-rpm.yaml` defines `Qualcomm Resource Power Manager (RPM) over SMD/GLINK`, a Qualcomm SoC inter-processor, power/resource-management, debug, or serial-engine binding. This driver is used to interface with the Resource Power Manager (RPM) found in various Qualcomm platforms. The RPM allows each component in the system to vote for state of the system resources, such as clocks, regulators and bus frequencies. The SMD or GLINK information for the RPM edge should be filled out. See qcom,smd.yaml for the required edge properties. All SMD/GLINK related properties will reside within the RPM node itself. The RPM exposes resources to its subnodes. The rpm_requests node must be present and this subnode may contain children that designate regulator resources. Refer to Documentation/devicetree/bindings/regulator/qcom,smd-rpm-regulator.yaml for information on the regulator subnodes that can exist under the rpm_requests. It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.
