# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,smem.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,smem.yaml` defines `Qualcomm Shared Memory Manager`, a Qualcomm SoC inter-processor, power/resource-management, debug, or serial-engine binding. This binding describes the Qualcomm Shared Memory Manager, a region of reserved-memory used to share data between various subsystems and OSes in Qualcomm platforms. It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. The schema uses `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/soc/qcom/qcom,smem.yaml#` and meta-schema `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses single `const` with 1 token: `qcom,smem`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `memory-region`, `hwlocks`, `qcom,rpm-msg-ram`, `no-map`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `hwlocks`; nested required-property signals include `compatible`, `hwlocks`, `reg`, `no-map`, `memory-region`. Pattern child-node APIs are none. Important resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `reg` describes MMIO or bus resource windows consumed during probe; `memory-region` binds reserved-memory or shared-memory backing. Collected limit and enum signals include `const=qcom,smem`, `maxItems=1`.

## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads this YAML, resolves `$ref` dependencies, validates the meta-schema, checks required properties, evaluates const/enum/items limits, applies top-level composition or conditionals (`oneOf`), validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. `dtbs_check` then applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. At runtime the YAML does not execute; OF/platform code matches `compatible`, maps MMIO resources, resolves phandles, registers providers or children, and lets the matched subsystem driver own the hardware behavior.

## State and Persistence Behavior
This file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data by itself. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: compatible strings, property names, phandle cell counts, child-node names, register ordering, clock/reset names, and examples become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Runtime state belongs to the matched kernel drivers and hardware, including Qualcomm RPM/RPMh, SMD, SMEM, SMP2P, SMSM, APR, GLINK, AOSS, GENI/QUP, GSBI, DCC, EUD, WCNSS, power, clock, interconnect, and mailbox drivers; the schema only constrains how that stateful hardware is described.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Andy Gross <agross@kernel.org>, Bjorn Andersson <bjorn.andersson@linaro.org>. Direct `$ref` dependencies are `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle`. Compatible scans found driver-side references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/mtd/parsers/qcomsmempart.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/of/platform.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/soc/qcom/smem.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/soc/qcom/smem_state.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/soc/qcom/smp2p.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/soc/qcom/smsm.c`; in-tree DTS/user references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/qcom/qcom-apq8064.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/qcom/qcom-ipq8064.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/qcom/qcom-msm8226.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/qcom/qcom-msm8974.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/qcom/qcom-sdx55.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/qcom/qcom-sdx65.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/agatti.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/qcom/eliza.dtsi`, and 2 more. The source has 98 lines and 2 embedded example blocks.

## Risks
Primary risks are DTS ABI drift around remoteproc/channel child nodes, mbox/interrupt semantics, shared-memory regions, RPM/RPMh resources, service IDs, clock/reset names, and compatible-specific branches; missing driver match-table updates when compatible strings change; register, interrupt, clock, reset, mailbox, memory-region, or power-domain ordering mismatches; and child-node regexes that admit malformed nodes or reject valid board descriptions. The closure profile rejects undeclared top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. Files with many compatible variants or conditionals need per-variant checks because one SoC fallback can validate while another silently loses required resources.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,smem.yaml` for targeted schema validation and example compilation, then `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,smem.yaml` on boards using these compatibles. High-value negative tests remove each required property, alter compatible fallback order, add an undeclared property when the schema is closed, break child-node names matched by `patternProperties`, and corrupt phandle cell counts. Runtime signals are successful probe of the matched SoC driver, expected provider registration, clean resource lookup, and no regressions in DTS users. Example seed: reserved-memory {; #address-cells = <1>;; #size-cells = <1>;; ranges;; ; smem@fa00000 {; compatible = "qcom,smem";; reg = <0xfa00000 0x200000>;; no-map;; ; hwlocks = <&tcsr_mutex 3>;; };; };;

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,smp2p.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,smp2p.yaml` defines `Qualcomm Shared Memory Point 2 Point`, a Qualcomm SoC inter-processor, power/resource-management, debug, or serial-engine binding. The Shared Memory Point to Point (SMP2P) protocol facilitates communication of a single 32-bit value between two processors. Each value has a single writer (the local side) and a single reader (the remote side). Values are uniquely identified in the system by the directed edge (local processor ID to remote processor ID) and a string identifier. It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. The schema uses `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/soc/qcom/qcom,smp2p.yaml#` and meta-schema `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses single `const` with 1 token: `qcom,smp2p`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `interrupts`, `mboxes`, `qcom,ipc`, `qcom,local-pid`, `qcom,remote-pid`, `qcom,smem`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `interrupts`, `qcom,local-pid`, `qcom,remote-pid`, `qcom,smem`; nested required-property signals include `qcom,entry-name`, `interrupt-controller`, `#interrupt-cells`, `#qcom,smem-state-cells`, `compatible`, `interrupts`, `qcom,local-pid`, `qcom,remote-pid`, `qcom,smem`, `mboxes`, `qcom,ipc`. Pattern child-node APIs are `^master-kernel|slave-kernel|ipa-ap-to-modem|ipa-modem-to-ap|wlan-ap-to-wpss|wlan-wpss-to-ap$`. Important resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `interrupts` maps hardware events into the IRQ domain; `mboxes` links the node to mailbox transport channels. Collected limit and enum signals include `const=qcom,smp2p`, `maxItems=1`, `maxItems=2`, `const=2`, `const=1`.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,smsm.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom,smsm.yaml` defines `Qualcomm Shared Memory State Machine`, a Qualcomm SoC inter-processor, power/resource-management, debug, or serial-engine binding. The Shared Memory State Machine facilitates broadcasting of single bit state information between the processors in a Qualcomm SoC. Each processor is assigned 32 bits of state that can be modified. A processor can through a matrix of bitmaps signal subscription of notifications upon changes to a certain bit owned by a certain remote processor. It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. The schema uses `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/soc/qcom/qcom,smsm.yaml#` and meta-schema `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses single `const` with 1 token: `qcom,smsm`. Pattern child-node APIs are `@[0-9a-f]$`, `^qcom,ipc-[1-4]$`. Important resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `#address-cells` controls child address translation and bus layout; `mboxes` links the node to mailbox transport channels; `#size-cells` controls child address translation and bus layout. Collected limit and enum signals include `const=qcom,smsm`, `const=1`, `minItems=1`, `maxItems=5`, `const=0`, `maxItems=1`, `const=2`.

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom-stats.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/qcom/qcom-stats.yaml` defines `Qualcomm Technologies, Inc. (QTI) Stats`, a Qualcomm SoC inter-processor, power/resource-management, debug, or serial-engine binding. Always On Processor/Resource Power Manager maintains statistics of the SoC sleep modes involving powering down of the rails and oscillator clock. Statistics includes SoC sleep mode type, number of times low power mode were entered, time of last entry, time of last exit and accumulated sleep duration. It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. The schema uses `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/soc/qcom/qcom-stats.yaml#` and meta-schema `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses `enum` list with 7 tokens: `qcom,rpmh-stats`, `qcom,sdm845-rpmh-stats`, `qcom,rpm-stats`, `qcom,apq8084-rpm-stats`, `qcom,msm8226-rpm-stats`, `qcom,msm8916-rpm-stats`, `qcom,msm8974-rpm-stats`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `qcom,qmp`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; nested required-property signals include `compatible`, `reg`. Pattern child-node APIs are none. The PWC IP found in the RZ/V2M family of chips comes with the below capabilities - external power supply on/off sequence generation - on/off signal generation for the LPDDR4 core power supply (LPVDD) - key input signals processing - general-purpose output pins It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. The schema uses `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/soc/renesas/renesas,rzv2m-pwc.yaml#` and meta-schema `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses ordered fallback `items` sequence with 3 tokens: `renesas,r9a09g011-pwc`, `renesas,r9a09g055-pwc`, `renesas,rzv2m-pwc`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `gpio-controller`, `#gpio-cells`, `renesas,rzv2m-pwc-power`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `gpio-controller`, `#gpio-cells`; nested required-property signals include `compatible`, `reg`, `gpio-controller`, `#gpio-cells`. Pattern child-node APIs are none. Important resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `reg` describes MMIO or bus resource windows consumed during probe; `#gpio-cells` exposes this node as a phandle provider. Collected limit and enum signals include `enum=[renesas,r9a09g011-pwc, renesas,r9a09g055-pwc]`, `const=renesas,rzv2m-pwc`, `maxItems=1`, `const=2`.

## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads this YAML, resolves `$ref` dependencies, validates the meta-schema, checks required properties, evaluates const/enum/items limits, applies top-level composition or conditionals (none), validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. `dtbs_check` then applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. At runtime the YAML does not execute; OF/platform code matches `compatible`, maps MMIO resources, resolves phandles, registers providers or children, and lets the matched subsystem driver own the hardware behavior.

## State and Persistence Behavior
This file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data by itself. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: compatible strings, property names, phandle cell counts, child-node names, register ordering, clock/reset names, and examples become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Runtime state belongs to the matched kernel drivers and hardware, including Renesas RZ/G, RZ/V, RZ/N, R-Car, system-controller, power-domain, reset, and SoC identification drivers; the schema only constrains how that stateful hardware is described.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Fabrizio Castro <fabrizio.castro.jz@renesas.com>. Direct `$ref` dependencies are dt-schema core/meta schemas only. Integration points include Renesas RZ/G, RZ/V, RZ/N, R-Car, system-controller, power-domain, reset, and SoC identification drivers. Compatible scans found driver-side references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/soc/renesas/pwc-rzv2m.c`; in-tree DTS/user references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/renesas/r9a09g011-v2mevk2.dts`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm64/boot/dts/renesas/r9a09g011.dtsi`. The source has 56 lines and 1 embedded example block.

## Risks
Primary risks are DTS ABI drift around root compatible ordering, system-controller register ranges, power-domain cells, interrupt mux wiring, and family-specific fallback strings; missing driver match-table updates when compatible strings change; register, interrupt, clock, reset, mailbox, memory-region, or power-domain ordering mismatches; and child-node regexes that admit malformed nodes or reject valid board descriptions. The closure profile rejects undeclared top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. Files with many compatible variants or conditionals need per-variant checks because one SoC fallback can validate while another silently loses required resources.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/renesas/renesas,rzv2m-pwc.yaml` for targeted schema validation and example compilation, then `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/renesas/renesas,rzv2m-pwc.yaml` on boards using these compatibles. High-value negative tests remove each required property, alter compatible fallback order, add an undeclared property when the schema is closed, break child-node names matched by `patternProperties`, and corrupt phandle cell counts. Runtime signals are successful probe of the matched SoC driver, expected provider registration, clean resource lookup, and no regressions in DTS users. Example seed: pwc: pwc@a3700000 {; compatible = "renesas,r9a09g011-pwc", "renesas,rzv2m-pwc";; reg = <0xa3700000 0x800>;; gpio-controller;; #gpio-cells = <2>;; renesas,rzv2m-pwc-power;; };;

Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; nested required-property signals include `compatible`, `reg`, `#clock-cells`, `clock-names`, `clocks`, `google,pmu-intr-gen-syscon`. Pattern child-node APIs are none. ## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads this YAML, resolves `$ref` dependencies, validates the meta-schema, checks required properties, evaluates const/enum/items limits, applies top-level composition or conditionals (`allOf`), validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. `dtbs_check` then applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. At runtime the YAML does not execute; OF/platform code matches `compatible`, maps MMIO resources, resolves phandles, registers providers or children, and lets the matched subsystem driver own the hardware behavior.

## State and Persistence Behavior
This file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data by itself. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: compatible strings, property names, phandle cell counts, child-node names, register ordering, clock/reset names, and examples become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Runtime state belongs to the matched kernel drivers and hardware, including Exynos PMU/syscon, USI serial-mode switching, power-management, clock/reset, and pinctrl integration; the schema only constrains how that stateful hardware is described.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Krzysztof Kozlowski <krzk@kernel.org>. Direct `$ref` dependencies are `/schemas/phy/samsung,dp-video-phy.yaml`, `/schemas/phy/samsung,mipi-video-phy.yaml`, `/schemas/power/reset/syscon-poweroff.yaml#`, `/schemas/power/reset/syscon-reboot-mode.yaml`, `/schemas/power/reset/syscon-reboot.yaml#`, `/schemas/types.yaml#/definitions/phandle`. Integration points include Exynos PMU/syscon, USI serial-mode switching, power-management, clock/reset, and pinctrl integration. Compatible scans found driver-side references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/ahci_imx.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/ahci_mtk.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/pata_ixp4xx_cf.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/ata/sata_gemini.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/auxdisplay/img-ascii-lcd.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bluetooth/ath3k.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bluetooth/btusb.c`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/drivers/bus/arm-integrator-lm.c`, and 2 more; in-tree DTS/user references in `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-a83t.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-h3.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-r40.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sun8i-v3s.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/allwinner/sunxi-h3-h5.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amazon/alpine.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amlogic/meson.dtsi`, `sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/arch/arm/boot/dts/amlogic/meson8.dtsi`, and 2 more. The source has 215 lines and 1 embedded example block.

## Risks
Primary risks are DTS ABI drift around syscon compatible fallbacks, clock names, mode-selection properties, register windows, and SoC generation compatibility; missing driver match-table updates when compatible strings change; register, interrupt, clock, reset, mailbox, memory-region, or power-domain ordering mismatches; and child-node regexes that admit malformed nodes or reject valid board descriptions. The closure profile rejects undeclared top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. Files with many compatible variants or conditionals need per-variant checks because one SoC fallback can validate while another silently loses required resources.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/samsung/exynos-pmu.yaml` for targeted schema validation and example compilation, then `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/samsung/exynos-pmu.yaml` on boards using these compatibles. High-value negative tests remove each required property, alter compatible fallback order, add an undeclared property when the schema is closed, break child-node names matched by `patternProperties`, and corrupt phandle cell counts. Runtime signals are successful probe of the matched SoC driver, expected provider registration, clean resource lookup, and no regressions in DTS users. Example seed: #include <dt-bindings/clock/exynos5250.h>; ; pmu_system_controller: system-controller@10040000 {; compatible = "samsung,exynos5250-pmu", "syscon";; reg = <0x10040000 0x5000>;; interrupt-controller;; #interrupt-cells = <3>;; interrupt-parent = <&gic>;; #cloc...

# sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/samsung/exynos-usi.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/samsung/exynos-usi.yaml` defines `Samsung's Exynos USI (Universal Serial Interface)`, a Samsung Exynos PMU, USI, or system-register binding. The USI IP-core provides configurable support for serial protocols, enabling different serial communication modes depending on the version. In USIv1, configurations are available to enable either one or two protocols simultaneously in select combinations - High-Speed I2C0, High-Speed I2C1, SPI, UART, High-Speed I2C0 and I2C1 or both High-Speed I2C1 and UART. In USIv2, only one protocol can be active at a time, either UART, SPI, or High-Speed I2C. The USI core shares internal circuits across protocols, meaning only the selected configuration is active at any given time. USI is modeled as a node with zero or more child nodes, each representing a serial sub-node device. The mode setting selects which particular function will be used. It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. The schema uses `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/soc/samsung/exynos-usi.yaml#` and meta-schema `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses `oneOf` with 2 accepted forms with 6 tokens: `google,gs101-usi`, `samsung,exynos2200-usi`, `samsung,exynosautov9-usi`, `samsung,exynosautov920-usi`, `samsung,exynos850-usi`, `samsung,exynos8895-usi`. Top-level properties are `$nodename`, `compatible`, `reg`, `clocks`, `clock-names`, `ranges`, `#address-cells`, `#size-cells`, `samsung,sysreg`, `samsung,mode`, `samsung,clkreq-on`. Pattern child-node APIs are `^i2c@[0-9a-f]+$`, `^serial@[0-9a-f]+$`, `^spi@[0-9a-f]+$`. Important resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `reg` describes MMIO or bus resource windows consumed during probe; `clocks` integrates with the clock framework; `clock-names` integrates with the clock framework; `ranges` controls child address translation and bus layout; `#address-cells` controls child address translation and bus layout; `#size-cells` controls child address translation and bus layout. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/socionext/socionext,uniphier-adamv.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/socionext/socionext,uniphier-adamv.yaml` defines `Socionext UniPhier ADAMV block`, a Socionext UniPhier SoC glue, controller, or peripheral-control binding. ADAMV block implemented on Socionext UniPhier SoCs is an analog signal amplifier that is a part of the external video and audio I/O system. This block is defined for controlling audio I/O reset only. It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. The schema uses `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/soc/socionext/socionext,uniphier-adamv.yaml#` and meta-schema `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses ordered fallback `items` sequence with 4 tokens: `socionext,uniphier-ld11-adamv`, `socionext,uniphier-ld20-adamv`, `simple-mfd`, `syscon`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `reset-controller`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; nested required-property signals include `compatible`, `reg`. Pattern child-node APIs are none. Important resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `reg` describes MMIO or bus resource windows consumed during probe. Collected limit and enum signals include `enum=[socionext,uniphier-ld11-adamv, socionext,uniphier-ld20-adamv]`, `const=simple-mfd`, `const=syscon`, `maxItems=1`.

## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads this YAML, resolves `$ref` dependencies, validates the meta-schema, checks required properties, evaluates const/enum/items limits, applies top-level composition or conditionals (none), validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. `dtbs_check` then applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. At runtime the YAML does not execute; OF/platform code matches `compatible`, maps MMIO resources, resolves phandles, registers providers or children, and lets the matched subsystem driver own the hardware behavior.

## State and Persistence Behavior
This file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data by itself. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: compatible strings, property names, phandle cell counts, child-node names, register ordering, clock/reset names, and examples become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Runtime state belongs to the matched kernel drivers and hardware, including UniPhier system glue, pinctrl, reset, clock, USB3, AHCI, SD, MIO, peripheral, ADAMV, and debug drivers; the schema only constrains how that stateful hardware is described.

## Dependencies and Integration Points
Maintainers: Kunihiko Hayashi <hayashi.kunihiko@socionext.com>. Direct `$ref` dependencies are `/schemas/reset/socionext,uniphier-reset.yaml#`. Integration points include UniPhier system glue, pinctrl, reset, clock, USB3, AHCI, SD, MIO, peripheral, ADAMV, and debug drivers. The source has 50 lines and 1 embedded example block.

## Risks
Primary risks are DTS ABI drift around syscon/control register ranges, child PHY or glue nodes, reset/clock providers, and UniPhier model-specific compatible strings; missing driver match-table updates when compatible strings change; register, interrupt, clock, reset, mailbox, memory-region, or power-domain ordering mismatches; and child-node regexes that admit malformed nodes or reject valid board descriptions. The closure profile rejects undeclared top-level properties with `additionalProperties: false`. Files with many compatible variants or conditionals need per-variant checks because one SoC fallback can validate while another silently loses required resources.

## Test Signals
Run `make dt_binding_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/socionext/socionext,uniphier-adamv.yaml` for targeted schema validation and example compilation, then `make dtbs_check DT_SCHEMA_FILES=Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/socionext/socionext,uniphier-adamv.yaml` on boards using these compatibles. High-value negative tests remove each required property, alter compatible fallback order, add an undeclared property when the schema is closed, break child-node names matched by `patternProperties`, and corrupt phandle cell counts. Runtime signals are successful probe of the matched SoC driver, expected provider registration, clean resource lookup, and no regressions in DTS users. Example seed: syscon@57920000 {; compatible = "socionext,uniphier-ld20-adamv",; "simple-mfd", "syscon";; reg = <0x57920000 0x1000>;; ; reset-controller {; compatible = "socionext,uniphier-ld20-adamv-reset";; #reset-cells = <1>;; };; };;

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/socionext/socionext,uniphier-mioctrl.yaml` defines `Socionext UniPhier media I/O block (MIO) controller`, a Socionext UniPhier SoC glue, controller, or peripheral-control binding. Media I/O block implemented on Socionext UniPhier SoCs is a legacy integrated component of the stream type peripherals including USB2.0, SD/eMMC, and MIO-DMAC. # sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/socionext/socionext,uniphier-perictrl.yaml

## Purpose
`sources/distributed-fs/ceph-client/Documentation/devicetree/bindings/soc/socionext/socionext,uniphier-perictrl.yaml` defines `Socionext UniPhier peripheral block controller`, a Socionext UniPhier SoC glue, controller, or peripheral-control binding. Peripheral block implemented on Socionext UniPhier SoCs is an integrated component of the peripherals including UART, I2C/FI2C, and SCSSI. Peripheral block controller is a logic to control the component. It is source-controlled ABI documentation and a dt-schema validation contract for board DTS/DTB data before Linux platform drivers consume that data.

## Important APIs, Types, and Functions
The public API is the Devicetree node shape, not callable functions. The schema uses `$id` `http://devicetree.org/schemas/soc/socionext/socionext,uniphier-perictrl.yaml#` and meta-schema `http://devicetree.org/meta-schemas/core.yaml#`. `compatible` uses ordered fallback `items` sequence with 11 tokens: `socionext,uniphier-ld4-perictrl`, `socionext,uniphier-pro4-perictrl`, `socionext,uniphier-pro5-perictrl`, `socionext,uniphier-pxs2-perictrl`, `socionext,uniphier-sld8-perictrl`, `socionext,uniphier-ld11-perictrl`, `socionext,uniphier-ld20-perictrl`, `socionext,uniphier-pxs3-perictrl`, `socionext,uniphier-nx1-perictrl`, `simple-mfd`, `syscon`. Top-level properties are `compatible`, `reg`, `clock-controller`, `reset-controller`. Top-level required properties are `compatible`, `reg`; nested required-property signals include `compatible`, `reg`. Pattern child-node APIs are none. Important resource/provider roles: `compatible` selects schema applicability and the kernel OF match-table entry; `reg` describes MMIO or bus resource windows consumed during probe. Collected limit and enum signals include `enum=[socionext,uniphier-ld4-perictrl, socionext,uniphier-pro4-perictrl, socionext,uniphier-pro5-perictrl, socionext,uniphier-pxs2-perictrl, socionext,uniphier-sld8-perictrl, socionext,uniphier-ld11-perictrl, socionext,uniphier-ld20-perictrl, socionext,uniphier-pxs3-perictrl, ...]`, `const=simple-mfd`, `const=syscon`, `maxItems=1`.

## Control Flow
Control flow is declarative schema evaluation. `dt_binding_check` loads this YAML, resolves `$ref` dependencies, validates the meta-schema, checks required properties, evaluates const/enum/items limits, applies top-level composition or conditionals (none), validates examples, and enforces the closure policy. `dtbs_check` then applies the same contract to compiled board DTBs. At runtime the YAML does not execute; OF/platform code matches `compatible`, maps MMIO resources, resolves phandles, registers providers or children, and lets the matched subsystem driver own the hardware behavior.

## State and Persistence Behavior
This file owns no mutable runtime state and persists no data by itself. Its persistent behavior is ABI stability: compatible strings, property names, phandle cell counts, child-node names, register ordering, clock/reset names, and examples become long-lived DTS/DTB contracts. Runtime state belongs to the matched kernel drivers and hardware, including UniPhier system glue, pinctrl, reset, clock, USB3, AHCI, SD, MIO, peripheral, ADAMV, and debug drivers; the schema only constrains how that stateful hardware is described.
